## 2026-08-05 — Full terminology system and configurable target reserve

**Decision:** Six settings rows — `term_expenses`, `term_income`, `term_projects`, `term_reconciliation`, `term_reserve` (migration 0019) and `site_name` (already seeded by migration 0014, left untouched) — are read together by a new `services/settings_service.get_terminology(db)` helper and exposed as a single object via `GET`/`PUT /settings/terminology`, rather than requiring the frontend to assemble them from the generic `GET /settings` list.
**Rationale:** The frontend needs all six as one atomic object on every app load (to render the Sidebar, Header, and page titles before anything else renders), and the task brief's "update one or more terminology settings at once" requirement doesn't fit the existing generic `PUT /settings/{key}` (one key per call). `GET /settings/terminology` is open to any authenticated user (not Admin-only) — matching the brief's explicit reasoning: every role needs these labels to render navigation, not just Admins who can change them.

**Decision:** `term_reserve` was added as a sixth editable field in the Settings page's Terminology section, even though Part 1's "Fields for each configurable term" bullet list named only five (Expenses, Income, Projects, Reconciliation, Site/instance name).
**Rationale:** `term_reserve` is explicitly one of the six settings the DATABASE section asks for, and Part 2's Target Reserve section says its "Label field... uses term_reserve from terminology (so it updates automatically when terminology is changed)" — implying the field is edited elsewhere and merely *displayed* there. Treating the five-item bullet list as incomplete (rather than deliberately excluding reserve-label editing from the app entirely) was judged the more coherent reading, and it's what makes all six seeded settings actually reachable from the UI.

**Decision:** The target reserve's "automatic" calculation keeps a fixed, non-configurable 3-month rolling-average window for "average monthly spend" (a new `financial_year_service.average_monthly_spend()`, factored out of the old `target_reserve()`), and only the **multiplier** (`reserve_months`, 1–12, default 3) is configurable — rather than making the averaging window itself the configurable "months" value.
**Rationale:** The original hardcoded behaviour was a 3-month average multiplied by... itself (used as both the window and the implicit "target = 3 months of spend"). The task brief's Part 2 wording — "target = rolling average monthly spend × reserve_months" and a Settings UI control explicitly labelled "months multiplier" — describes two distinct numbers, not one. Decoupling them means changing the multiplier to 6 targets six months of reserve without changing what "average monthly spend" means, and reproduces the exact original behaviour when left at its default (3-month window × 3 months multiplier = the old formula, unchanged for anyone who doesn't touch the new setting).

**Decision:** `target_reserve(db, today)` now returns a `(Decimal, dict)` tuple — the amount plus the resolved `{calculation, months, manual_amount}` settings — instead of a bare `Decimal`, so `financial_year_service.build_summary()` (its only caller) can report `reserve_calculation`/`reserve_months` in `/dashboard/summary` without a second settings read.
**Rationale:** The task brief requires the summary response to include the calculation method and months multiplier alongside the amount. Reading the settings twice (once inside `target_reserve`, once again in `build_summary`) risked the two reads disagreeing if a value changed between them; returning the resolved settings from the one read avoids that.

**Decision:** `GET /dashboard/notifications`'s balance-warning messages and unreviewed/unsigned-invoice messages now interpolate the live `term_reserve`/`term_expenses` settings (lower-cased, with a small `_singularize()` helper for count-based phrasing) instead of the hardcoded words "target reserve" and "invoice(s)".
**Rationale:** These notification banners are backend-generated strings, not frontend components — the frontend can't retroactively rename a word baked into a string it just renders verbatim. Since the backend already has DB access via `settings_service`, having it speak the organisation's configured vocabulary was the only way to satisfy the task's "notification banners must use the hook instead of a hardcoded label" requirement for banners whose text originates server-side.

**Decision:** The frontend's `TerminologyContext` (`frontend/src/context/TerminologyContext.jsx`) only fetches `GET /settings/terminology` when a user is logged in (`user?.token` present); pre-login routes (Login, Setup, Register) see the hardcoded `TERMINOLOGY_DEFAULTS` instead of a fetch attempt.
**Rationale:** The endpoint requires authentication (any logged-in user, not anonymous), and none of the pre-login pages render the Sidebar/Header/Layout that actually display these labels — there's nothing for a pre-login fetch to accomplish, and attempting one would just log a spurious 401 on every login/setup page load.

**Decision:** `TerminologyContext` exposes a `loading` flag distinct from its `term_*` values (which are always populated, defaulting to `TERMINOLOGY_DEFAULTS` before the fetch resolves), and the Settings page's Terminology form only seeds its editable inputs from context once `loading` is `false`.
**Rationale:** Without this, the Terminology form would briefly seed itself from the placeholder defaults (identical in shape to real data, so nothing would visibly fail) and then never re-sync once the real fetch landed a moment later — an Admin editing quickly after page load could unknowingly overwrite a genuinely-customised label back to its default on save.

**Decision:** Best-effort singular helpers (`frontend/src/utils/format.js`'s `singularize()`, and a near-identical private `_singularize()` in `backend/routers/dashboard.py`) strip a trailing `-ies`/`-s` to turn a configured plural label into a singular one for phrases like "Upload invoice" / "1 invoice still waiting" — duplicated rather than shared across the frontend/backend language boundary.
**Rationale:** Every configured term ships as a plural noun ("Invoices," "Contributions"), but several UI strings need the singular ("+ Upload invoice," "Untitled invoice," a count of exactly 1). A plain suffix-stripping heuristic isn't linguistically perfect for every conceivable custom term an Admin might type, but it correctly handles the shipped defaults and any ordinary plural rename, which was judged sufficient without adding a pluralisation library for either language.

**Decision:** Not every occurrence of the words "invoice"/"contribution"/"project"/"reconciliation" in the frontend was converted to use the terminology hook — only the categories the task brief explicitly listed (sidebar links, page titles/headings, notification banners, dashboard panel headings, empty states, breadcrumbs/back links) plus the small set of quick-action buttons and loading states needed for those pages to read consistently. Inline form field labels (e.g. "Invoice date," "Discrepancy notes"), generic error-fallback strings (e.g. `catch` block messages), and descriptive body copy (e.g. Projects page's "a project, a purchase, a repair") were left as-is.
**Rationale:** Converting every single occurrence across the whole codebase — including internal error-handling strings never normally seen by a user — was judged disproportionate scope creep against a task that gave an explicit, bounded list of what must be converted. The boundary drawn here (headings/navigation/empty-states/banners: converted; incidental prose and error fallbacks: left alone) keeps the change reviewable and matches what the brief actually asked for.

**Tested:** Verified against the running dev containers. Ran `alembic upgrade head` (migrations 0019, 0020) cleanly against the live dev database; restarted the backend and confirmed `/health` still returns `200`. Exercised the API directly via `curl` as Superadmin: `GET /settings/terminology` returned the seeded defaults; `PUT /settings/terminology` with a partial payload (renaming expenses to "Bills", income to "Income", reserve to "Rainy Day Fund", site name to "KHOC") updated only those fields and left `term_projects`/`term_reconciliation` at their defaults; `GET /dashboard/summary` immediately reflected `reserve_label: "Rainy Day Fund"`; `GET /dashboard/notifications` immediately reflected the renamed label in its balance-warning message text ("...is below the rainy day fund (£0.00)."). Switched `reserve_calculation` to `manual` with `reserve_manual_amount=5000.00` — `target_reserve` in the summary became `5000.00` with `reserve_months: null`; switched back to `automatic` with `reserve_months=6` — `target_reserve` recalculated from the 3-month rolling average × 6. Confirmed `PUT /settings/reserve_calculation` with an invalid value and `PUT /settings/reserve_months` with `13` both correctly return `400`, and that both `GET` and `PUT /settings/terminology` return `401` with no token. Restored all settings to their defaults afterwards; no other data was left changed. Built the frontend production bundle (`vite build`) twice — clean before and after the final edits — and watched the dev container's Vite HMR log accept every edited file (Sidebar, Header, DashboardPage, InvoicesPage, ContributionsPage, ReconciliationPage, ProjectsPage, ForecastBreakdownPage, UploadPage, InvoiceDetailPage, SettingsPage, TerminologyContext) with no compile errors.

Also verified visually in a real browser (system Chrome, driven headlessly via `playwright-core`, since no browser tool was bundled in this session — Chrome was already installed on the host, so no browser download was needed) logged in as Superadmin: on the Settings page, filled the Terminology form (Expenses → "Bills," Income → "Income," Reserve → "Rainy Day Fund," Site name → "KHOC") and watched the live-preview sidebar mock update on every keystroke; clicked **Save all** and confirmed the *real* Sidebar and Header updated immediately, with no page reload (`Bills`/`Income`/`Keep Track — KHOC` visible in a screenshot taken straight after the save click). On the Target Reserve section, switched to **Manual**, entered £4,321, saved, then navigated to the Dashboard and confirmed the gauge showed "Rainy Day Fund" as its title, "Balance £-713.07 / Target £4,321.00," and "Manually set target" beneath it — and that the quick-action buttons read "+ Upload bill" / "+ Record income" (`singularize()` applied correctly to the renamed terms) and the balance-below-target notification banner read "...is below the rainy day fund (£4,321.00)." Used **Reset to defaults** (with its confirmation step) and switched the reserve back to Automatic to restore the dev database exactly as found. The only browser console error observed across the whole run was a pre-existing, unrelated `favicon.ico` 404 (confirmed present before this feature's changes too, via a direct `curl` to `/favicon.ico`) — no errors from the terminology/reserve code itself. One test-script bug was caught and fixed along the way: an initial unscoped `button:has-text("Save")` selector matched the *App start date* row's Save button (which appears earlier in the DOM) instead of the Target Reserve section's, silently saving the wrong thing — fixed by scoping the locator to the Target Reserve section's own container; this was a bug in the verification script, not the app.

## 2026-08-05 — Two-panel Settings restructure and user profile page

**Decision:** `SettingsPage.jsx` was split into a thin two-panel shell plus one component per category under a new `frontend/src/components/settings/` directory (`SettingsNav`, `GeneralSettings`, `TerminologySettings`, `SigningExportSettings`, `UsersSettings`, `ComingSoonSettings`, `DangerZoneSettings`). Each settings-bearing section (`GeneralSettings`, `SigningExportSettings`) fetches its own copy of `GET /settings` on mount rather than the page lifting one shared list into every child, and `TerminologySettings` reads/writes via the existing `TerminologyContext` exactly as the old single-file page did.
**Rationale:** The task brief's category list (General, Terminology, Signing & Export, AI & Extraction, Storage & Backup, Users, Notifications, Danger Zone) maps naturally to one component per category, and keeping each section's data-loading local avoids a shared-state re-sync bug: the previous single-file page already needed a "seeded" flag to stop the Terminology form re-copying from a still-loading context on every render (see the terminology build entry above) — giving `GeneralSettings` and `SigningExportSettings` their own independent fetch keeps that same class of bug from spreading across sections that switch out from under each other as the active category changes. The extra `GET /settings` calls are inexpensive (a handful of rows, Admin-only, called only when that category is actually opened).

**Decision:** The "Categories" management link (previously a flat settings row) was placed inside the new **General** category, even though the task brief's category list doesn't mention category management at all.
**Rationale:** The brief enumerates what each new category should contain but never says what happens to the pre-existing "Manage categories" link — dropping it from the UI entirely would be a silent regression of existing, working functionality (`/settings/categories`, already Admin-gated and unaffected by this restructure). General was judged the least-bad fit among the eight given categories.

**Decision:** The **Users** category is not a "Coming soon" placeholder like AI & Extraction / Storage & Backup / Notifications / Danger Zone. It renders real content: the list of not-yet-approved registrations (`GET /auth/pending-users`) with a role dropdown and an **Approve** button (`POST /auth/approve-user/{id}`) — both endpoints already existed (see the 2026-08-04 auth build entry) but had no frontend consumer anywhere in the app before this task.
**Rationale:** The brief's own wording singles Users out — "link to user management" — distinct from the explicit "'Coming soon' message" instruction given to every other placeholder category, so treating it as a sixth placeholder would contradict the brief. Building a full user-management page (editing existing users' roles, deactivating accounts) was judged out of scope here — the task's own BACKEND section only asked for self-service `/auth/me/*` endpoints, nothing about managing *other* users — so this stays scoped to the one piece of already-built-but-unwired backend functionality (pending-user approval) that fits "user management" without inventing new endpoints mid-task.

**Decision:** Mobile behaviour (category list first, tap slides to a content panel with a back arrow) is implemented as a CSS-only sliding track — `.kt-settings-track` is a flex row at double width (`200%`) with the nav and content panels each pinned to 50%, translated via `transform: translateX(-50%)` when a `.show-content` class is present on the shell — rather than mounting/unmounting the two panels or animating with JS.
**Rationale:** Keeps the "smooth transition" requirement working with a single CSS transition and no extra state beyond the `mobileShowContent` boolean already needed to decide which panel to show; matches the project's established minimal-dependency, hand-rolled-CSS pattern (no animation library, same as everywhere else in the app).

**Decision:** `users.display_name` (`VARCHAR(150)`, nullable, migration `0021`) was added. `UserOut` now includes it, and every place a user's name is shown (Header, Profile page, the Users approval list is unaffected since it shows pending *registrations* which never have one yet) falls back to `username` when it's unset.
**Rationale:** The task brief's profile page explicitly needs an editable "display name" distinct from the immutable username, and no such column existed. Nullable because every existing user (including the seeded Superadmin) has none yet, and the UI's `user.display_name || user.username` fallback (`Header.jsx`, `ProfilePage.jsx`) means a blank value is never shown as literally blank.

**Decision:** `PUT /auth/me/profile` and `PUT /auth/me/password` (`backend/routers/auth.py`) both use the existing `get_current_user` dependency, not `require_admin` or `require_standard` — any authenticated, approved role (including Read Only) can change their own name, email, or password.
**Rationale:** These endpoints act only on the caller's own row (`user` from the dependency, never a client-supplied id), so there is no cross-user privilege question — restricting a Read Only account from managing its own login credentials would contradict every other "acts on my own account" flow in the app (e.g. `GET /auth/me` itself has no role gate).

**Decision:** `PUT /auth/me/profile` checks email uniqueness with `User.email == payload.email, User.id != user.id` (i.e. excluding the caller's own current row) before accepting the change, matching the pattern already used by `/auth/setup` and `/auth/register` for new-account email uniqueness.
**Rationale:** Without excluding the caller's own id, a user resubmitting their own unchanged email would incorrectly be told it's "already registered" — the constraint needs to mean "taken by *someone else*," not "already exists."

**Decision:** `AuthContext` gained a `refreshUser()` method (re-fetches `GET /auth/me` and replaces the stored `user`, keeping the existing token) that `ProfilePage` calls after a successful profile save, rather than the page reconstructing the updated user object itself from its own form state.
**Rationale:** `GET /auth/me` is the single source of truth for what the backend actually persisted (e.g. a trimmed/normalised value); re-fetching guarantees the Header's live display name and any other `useAuth()` consumer immediately reflect exactly what's in the database, not what the form optimistically assumed was saved.

**Decision:** The Header's username/logout controls were replaced with a single dropdown button (`.kt-header-profile-button`) showing `user.display_name || user.username` with a caret, opened by click and closed by an outside-click listener (`useEffect` + `mousedown` on `document`), containing "My profile" (→ `/profile`) and "Sign out" — rather than a native `<details>/<summary>` element.
**Rationale:** `<details>` would have needed non-trivial extra CSS to suppress its default disclosure-triangle marker and to close on outside click/route navigation (React Router's `Link` doesn't close a native `<details>` automatically), whereas the state+listener approach the app already uses elsewhere (e.g. `ProjectCard`'s confirm-step pattern) composes cleanly with `useNavigate()` for the sign-out redirect.

**Tested:** Verified against the running dev containers (an `alembic upgrade head` was required to apply migration `0021`, and the backend container needed a manual restart to pick it up plus the new router code — this project's backend has no `--reload` flag, unlike Vite's frontend HMR which picked up every frontend file automatically). Confirmed `GET /openapi.json` listed the two new routes only after the restart. Ran `vite build` — clean, no errors. Verified visually in a real browser (system Chrome via `playwright-core`, headless) logged in as Superadmin: screenshotted all eight Settings categories on desktop (1280px) — General showed the moved App start date/Financial year/Categories/Target reserve rows exactly as before restructuring; Danger Zone rendered in red in both the nav link and the content panel; Users showed "Admin" badge in the nav. Resized to 400px and confirmed the mobile view starts on the category list, tapping "Terminology" slides to its content panel with a working "← Back to Settings" link that returns to the list. On the Profile page: changed the display name, saved, and confirmed both the inline success message and the *Header* updated immediately to the new name with no page reload; reverted it back to blank and confirmed the database column returned to `NULL` (not empty string) via a direct `psql` check. Attempted a password change with a deliberately wrong current password — got the inline "Current password is incorrect" error (`400`), then successfully changed the password with the correct current one and changed it back afterwards, confirming the new password worked as the "current" password on the second call. Registered a throwaway user (`qa_throwaway_user`) via `/register`, confirmed it appeared in Settings → Users, approved it with the default "Standard user" role via the panel's dropdown + button, and confirmed both that it disappeared from the pending list and that the database row showed `role='standard', approved=true`; the throwaway user was deleted from the database afterwards. The only console error observed throughout was the pre-existing, unrelated `favicon.ico` 404 already noted in earlier entries in this log.

## 2026-08-05 — Frontend tidy-up pass 2: sidebar cleanup, copyright footer, dropdown font fix

**Decision:** The "Categories" sub-link was removed from under Settings in `Sidebar.jsx` (and the now-unused sublist/child rendering branch and its `.kt-nav-sublist`/`.kt-nav-sublink` CSS deleted), rather than just hiding it. The `/settings/categories` route itself was left untouched.
**Rationale:** `GeneralSettings.jsx` already has its own "Manage categories" button linking to the same route (added in an earlier settings-restructure pass), so the sidebar entry was a redundant second path to the same page. Leaving the route in place means the button (and any bookmarked/direct link) still works — only the now-duplicate nav entry was removed.

**Decision:** The new copyright footer lives in a `<Footer />` component rendered once at the top of `App.jsx`, wrapping the entire `<Routes>` tree in a new `.kt-app-shell` / `.kt-app-content` flex-column pair, rather than being added separately inside `Layout.jsx` (for the authenticated app) and `AuthCard.jsx` (for login/register/MFA/setup). `Layout.jsx`'s own `.kt-app` and `AuthCard.jsx`'s `.kt-auth-screen` (and the shared `.kt-auth-loading` spinner screen) were changed from `min-height: 100vh` to `flex: 1; min-height: 0` so they fill the remaining space above the footer instead of each independently claiming a full viewport height.
**Rationale:** The task required the footer on every page, including screens that don't go through `Layout.jsx` at all (login, register, MFA, the setup wizard). A single component at the `App.jsx` root guarantees that with no risk of two copies drifting apart, and reusing the existing internal-scroll pattern (`kt-main` already scrolls within a fixed-height shell) meant the footer could sit below the sidebar/main content without breaking that scroll behaviour or requiring page-level scrolling to reach it.

**Decision:** Added a `--kt-text-tertiary` CSS variable (`#9A9A95`) to `theme.css` for the footer text colour, rather than reusing one of the existing ad-hoc muted greys (`#6b6b67`, `#8a8a86`) already scattered inline/in component CSS across the app.
**Rationale:** The task brief suggested `--color-text-tertiary` "or similar"; no tertiary text variable existed yet (only `--kt-text` for primary), and the existing muted greys are undocumented one-off values rather than named tokens. A dedicated variable is lighter than both, reads as clearly subordinate to body text, and gives future muted-text needs something to reuse instead of another inline hex.

**Decision:** The dropdown font-size fix was made in three layers rather than one: (1) a global `input, select, textarea { font-family: inherit; font-size: 15px; }` baseline added to `theme.css`, (2) the existing shared `.kt-field input` rule in `auth.css` (padding/border/font, used by every labelled form field across the app) was extended to also match `.kt-field select` and `.kt-field textarea`, and (3) a new `.kt-settings-row-control select` rule was added to `settings.css` for the two selects that live outside a `.kt-field` wrapper (the Users role picker and the Settings financial-year-start-month picker).
**Rationale:** Investigation found the root cause: `.kt-field input` had only ever styled `<input>` elements, so every `<select>` sitting inside a `.kt-field` div (the invoice review card's category dropdown, the Invoices page filters, Contributions/Projects forms, the setup wizard) silently fell back to the browser's native select styling — a different font entirely, not just a different size. Extending the one shared rule fixed every `.kt-field`-wrapped select at once; the global element-level rule is a safety net for any select/input/textarea that isn't (or won't be) wrapped in `.kt-field`; the two remaining bare selects needed their own explicit rule since they use a different wrapper class.

**Tested:** Ran `vite build` — clean, no errors. Verified against the running dev containers (Vite HMR picked up all changes live) with Playwright (Chromium, headless) logged in as Superadmin: confirmed the sidebar lists exactly Dashboard/Invoices/Contributions/Reconciliation/Projects/Reports/Settings with no Categories entry or sub-list, and that navigating directly to `/settings/categories` still loads the page. Confirmed the footer ("© 2026 Keep Track. All rights reserved.") renders correctly positioned — thin top border, centred, subtle colour — on the login page (logged out) and on the Dashboard, Invoices, and Settings pages (logged in), without pushing the sidebar/header layout around or breaking `kt-main`'s internal scroll. Read back computed styles via `getComputedStyle` for every `<select>` on the Invoices and Settings pages and confirmed each now matches the app's body font stack and a consistent size, where before the fix at least one (the Settings financial-year select) rendered in the browser's default UI font. No console errors were observed during any of the above.

## 2026-08-05 — System reset with Superadmin protection

**Decision:** The DELETE order/table list in this task's brief includes `notifications`, but no such table exists anywhere in this codebase — `database-schema.md` documents one, but the main dashboard build (see that entry above, 2026-08-04) deliberately computes dashboard notifications live from other tables instead of building it. `perform_reset()` (`backend/services/system_reset_service.py`) simply has nothing to delete from and doesn't reference the table at all.
**Rationale:** Matches this log's established practice of treating a documented-but-never-built table as a no-op rather than inventing a migration for a table nothing else in the app reads or writes, purely to satisfy one line of this task's table list.

**Decision:** `DEFAULT_CATEGORIES` (the same 7 rows as migration `0003`) and `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` (the full set of rows seeded across migrations `0007`, `0014`, `0015`, `0018`, `0019`, `0020` — 12 keys in total) are duplicated as constants in `services/system_reset_service.py` rather than imported from the migration files themselves.
**Rationale:** A runtime reseed and a one-off migration-time seed are different operations that happen to want identical starting data; importing from `alembic/versions/` would couple runtime code to migration internals that are free to be squashed or rewritten later. Matches the precedent `services/settings_service.py`'s `TERMINOLOGY_DEFAULTS` already set for exactly this situation (its own comment: "Defaults match the seeded values in migration 0019 — used as the fallback... same pattern as `is_signing_enabled`").

**Decision:** Rate limiting (3 attempts/hour) is implemented by querying the `system_events` table itself — `recent_attempt_count()` counts `system_reset_success`/`system_reset_failed` rows for that `performed_by` in the trailing hour — rather than adding a rate-limiting library or any in-memory counter. A request rejected purely for being rate-limited is logged under a third, separate event type (`system_reset_rate_limited`) that is deliberately excluded from that count.
**Rationale:** Matches the project's established minimal-dependency convention (no new packages for something a query over data already being written can do), and reuses infrastructure the task already required (the permanent audit log) rather than adding a second, parallel tracking mechanism. Excluding `rate_limited` events from the count specifically prevents a locked-out caller from extending their own lockout by hammering the endpoint — each such request adds a new timestamp to the window only if it were counted, which would otherwise never let the window expire while attempts kept arriving. Verified directly: three wrong-password attempts as Superadmin correctly returned `403` each time and were logged as `system_reset_failed`; a fourth immediately returned `429` and was logged as `system_reset_rate_limited`; a throwaway Standard user's own attempt in between was correctly *not* counted against the Superadmin's limit, since counting is scoped by `performed_by`.

**Decision:** The Superadmin-only check (`user.role != "superadmin"`) is written inline in `routers/system.py`'s handler body, not as a reusable `require_superadmin` FastAPI dependency (contrast `require_admin`/`require_standard` in `utils/deps.py`).
**Rationale:** A dependency raises before the endpoint body runs, which would make it impossible to log a rejected non-Superadmin attempt to `system_events` — and the task brief requires logging "every attempt (successful or not)." Doing the check inline still satisfies the security requirement to verify "role = 'superadmin' on the database row, not just the JWT claim," since `user` here is the same freshly-DB-loaded object `get_current_user` already builds for every other endpoint in the app (nothing in this app makes authorization decisions from unverified JWT claims — the dependency always re-reads the row).

**Decision:** Files are deleted from the real, already-configured storage locations — `settings.invoice_storage_path` + `/original`, `settings.signed_invoice_storage_path`, and `settings.report_storage_path` — rather than the `/app/storage/invoices/` and `/app/storage/reports/` paths named in the task brief.
**Rationale:** Identical reasoning to every prior storage-path deviation in this log (original/signed invoice storage, report storage): those are the paths actually mounted as persistent Docker volumes per `docker-compose.yml`; an unmounted `/app/storage` path was never real.

**Decision:** `_wipe_uploaded_files()` clears each storage directory's *contents* (`os.scandir` + per-entry `os.remove`/`shutil.rmtree`) rather than `shutil.rmtree`-ing the directory itself and recreating it.
**Rationale:** Caught by testing the real `wipe_files=true` path against the running dev containers, not by inspection: the first version called `shutil.rmtree(base_dir)` on all three storage roots, which crashed the request with `OSError: [Errno 16] Device or resource busy: '/data/reports'` — `report_storage_path` (`/data/reports`) is itself a Docker volume's mount point (see `docker-compose.yml`), and the kernel won't let you unlink a mount root, only empty it. `invoice_storage_path` (`/data/invoices`) is a mount point too, though the crash happened on `/data/reports` first since it's later in iteration order. The database portion of the reset had already committed by the time this crashed (file wiping is deliberately the last step, see `perform_reset`), so the failure only ever left stale files behind — never partial or inconsistent data — but it did surface as a `500`/"Failed to fetch" in the browser instead of a clean success. Fixed and re-verified: created dummy files under all three storage paths, re-ran a reset with `wipe_files=true`, confirmed every dummy file was gone and all three directories remained present, empty, and writable (no restart needed).

**Decision:** `models.system_event.SystemEvent.details` uses `JSONB`, matching `reports.categories_included`/`parameters`'s precedent (see the AI report generation build entry above) rather than a normalised set of columns.
**Rationale:** Each event's payload shape varies by `event_type` (a rejected attempt logs a `reason` string; a rate-limited one doesn't; a successful reset logs only `wipe_files`) — the same "arbitrary, growing set of fields, never queried by field, only read back whole" situation that justified `JSONB` for reports' own metadata.

**Decision:** On the frontend, a successful reset calls the existing `logout()` (clearing the stored JWT) and then does a hard `window.location.href = '/setup'` — not React Router's client-side `navigate()`.
**Rationale:** Every piece of in-memory app state (`AuthContext`'s `user`/`setupRequired`, `TerminologyContext`'s cached labels, any component still holding fetched lists) describes a system that the reset just deleted. `setupRequired` in particular is only ever set once, at `AuthProvider`'s initial bootstrap — a client-side navigation to `/setup` would hit `RequireSetupNeeded`'s guard using the *stale* `setupRequired: false` still held in memory from before the reset and immediately redirect back to `/login`. A full reload re-runs that bootstrap from scratch against the now-actually-reset backend.

**Decision:** `DangerZoneSettings.jsx` renders the full reset UI only when `user.role === 'superadmin'`; any other Admin who reaches Settings → Danger Zone (the page itself, and the Danger Zone nav entry, both remain governed by the existing `RequireAdmin` route guard covering all of Settings) sees a short "System reset is only available to the Superadmin account" message instead.
**Rationale:** The task brief requires the feature to be "only accessible when logged in as Superadmin," but Settings as a whole — and every other Danger Zone-adjacent nav entry — has always been Admin-gated, not Superadmin-gated (see the 2026-08-04 PDF signing workflow build entry: "the whole page is Admin-only"). Narrowing the *page* to Superadmin-only would be a bigger, unrequested change; narrowing just the reset UI inside the existing Danger Zone panel satisfies the requirement precisely.

**Tested:** Verified against the running dev containers (`alembic upgrade head` applied migration `0022` cleanly; the backend container was restarted to pick up the new router and, later, the file-wipe fix — this project's backend has no `--reload`). Exercised every rejection path directly via `curl` as Superadmin: wrong password → `403 Incorrect Superadmin password.`; wrong confirmation phrase → `403` naming the exact required phrase; a throwaway Standard user (minted directly in the database, JWT signed with the backend's own `create_token` helper) → `403 Superadmin privileges required`; three real wrong-password attempts followed by a fourth → `429 Too many reset attempts...`. Inspected `system_events` after each and confirmed every attempt was logged with the right `event_type`/`performed_by`/`details`, and that the Standard user's rejected attempt didn't count against the Superadmin's own rate limit. Cleared those test-only log rows and the throwaway user via `psql` before the real end-to-end pass (consistent with this log's existing throwaway-data-cleanup convention), so the genuine test below wasn't itself blocked by the rate limit it had just proven works.

Then ran the full flow through a real browser (system Chrome, headless via `playwright-core`, same approach as prior sessions) logged in as Superadmin: opened Settings → Danger Zone and confirmed the red warning box lists exactly the required seven deletion bullets; confirmed **Reset all data** starts disabled; toggled **Wipe files** on and saw the additional file-loss warning appear; typed an incorrect phrase and confirmed the password field stayed hidden and the button stayed disabled; typed `RESET KEEP TRACK` and confirmed the password field appeared; submitted a deliberately wrong password and saw the inline `Incorrect Superadmin password.` error while remaining on the Settings page; then submitted the correct password and confirmed the app redirected to `/setup`. A separate, fresh, fully unauthenticated visit to `/` afterwards landed directly on the Setup wizard ("Welcome to Keep Track — Let's create the first Admin account to get started"), confirming `GET /auth/setup-status` now correctly reports `setup_required: true`. Queried the database directly and confirmed: all 7 default categories and all 12 default settings (including `app_start_date`/`reserve_manual_amount` correctly `NULL`, not a sentinel string) were restored exactly; `invoices`, `invoice_files`, `contributions`, `monthly_reconciliations`, `planned_projects`, `reports`, and `financial_years` were all empty; `users` contained only the Superadmin row; `system_events` retained the full history of every attempt made during this testing, including the successful resets themselves. Confirmed uploaded-file wiping separately (see the file-wipe bug entry above) by planting dummy files under all three storage paths and re-running a reset with `wipe_files: true`, then confirming every dummy file was gone while the storage directories themselves remained present and writable. No unexpected browser console errors were observed (the only one seen was the pre-existing, unrelated `favicon.ico` 404 already noted in earlier entries in this log).

**Note on dev data:** This is destructive by design, and the task explicitly required testing the real reset end-to-end. Running it against the shared dev database means the data accumulated across every prior feature build in this log — 9 invoices, 4 contributions, 4 monthly reconciliations, 3 planned projects, 6 reports, 4 financial years, and the pre-existing `richard` Admin account — was genuinely and permanently deleted as a direct result of this testing, exactly as the feature is designed to do. Only the Superadmin account survived. Anyone continuing to use this dev environment will need to go through the Setup wizard again to create a new Admin account; there is no prior state to restore.

## 2026-08-05 — Full-screen signing UI and coordinate-mapping fix

**Decision:** Root-caused the reported "coordinate mapping is broken" bug to `SigningPanel.jsx`'s canvas CSS, not to the backend. Every PDF page canvas was rendered at a fixed pdf.js resolution (`renderPdfPage(..., scale=1.1)`, i.e. `canvas.width`/`.height` in that native resolution) but then displayed with `.kt-sign-canvas { max-width: 100% }` — on any viewport narrower than that native resolution (which, at scale 1.1, was most of them), the browser shrank the canvas visually while the drag/percentage math still worked in the canvas's *native* pixel space. Pointer deltas (`e.clientX`/`e.clientY`, real screen pixels) were then applied 1:1 to that native-pixel space, so the box moved faster or slower than the cursor and visibly drifted from wherever the user actually dragged it. `backend/services/signing_service.py` and `InvoiceSignRequest` were already fully percentage-based and already used `page.rect` (confirmed directly with a throwaway `fitz` script: a page rotated 90° reports `page.rect` as `100×200` for a `200×100` mediabox, and `get_pixmap()` renders at that same rotated size) — so no backend change was needed for Part 2 of this task; the fix is entirely in how the frontend renders and measures the PDF.

**Decision:** `renderPdfPage` (`frontend/src/utils/pdf.js`) now takes a `targetWidth` (CSS pixels) instead of a fixed `scale`, renders the backing canvas at `targetWidth * devicePixelRatio` for crispness, but pins `canvas.style.width`/`.height` to the CSS-pixel size and returns that same CSS-pixel `{width, height}` to the caller. `SigningPanel` measures its own available width via a `ResizeObserver` on the scroll container and re-renders every page to fill exactly that width — the `max-width: 100%` CSS rule was removed entirely, since the canvas is now always rendered at the size it's displayed at, not shrunk into it after the fact. This is what makes the percentage math (and the box's on-screen position) match the visually rendered PDF at any viewport size, including live window resizes.
**Rationale:** Once the canvas's native resolution and its CSS-displayed size are the same number, `e.clientX`/`e.clientY`-based pointer deltas are already in the correct coordinate space with no scaling correction needed — the simplest fix available, and it also naturally makes the redesigned full-screen layout (PDF width now varies with viewport/toolbar-drawer state, not fixed) responsive without extra math.

**Decision:** `SigningPanel` was rewritten to render into a `createPortal(..., document.body)` full-screen overlay (`position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: 1000`) instead of a two-column grid embedded inside `ReviewCard`/`InvoiceDetailPage`. Both call sites were changed to `return <SigningPanel .../>` directly (an early return) rather than nesting it inside their existing card/page markup, since the portal makes that wrapper invisible-but-still-mounted dead weight.
**Rationale:** Directly implements this task's "takes over the full browser window... the rest of the app UI hidden" requirement. A portal to `document.body` is the simplest way to guarantee that coverage regardless of which page (`ReviewCard`'s upload flow, or `InvoiceDetailPage`'s standalone sign/re-sign flow) opened it, with no change needed to either page's own layout/z-index — consistent with the project's "no component library, plain browser APIs" pattern already established for the drag/resize logic itself.

**Decision:** The floating toolbar and its mobile "bottom drawer collapsible to a tab" behaviour are pure CSS (a `kt-sign-toolbar-collapsed` class toggling `transform: translateY(calc(100% - 44px))` under a `max-width: 800px` media query, matching the breakpoint the old two-column signing grid already used) rather than a JS-driven animation or a second component.
**Rationale:** Matches the same CSS-only sliding-panel pattern already used for the Settings page's mobile nav/content transition (see the 2026-08-05 Settings restructure entry above) — no new dependency, one boolean of state (`drawerOpen`), and the collapsed tab stays interactive (still a real button, not `display: none`) so re-expanding it is instant.

**Decision:** All four corner resize handles (`nw`/`ne`/`sw`/`se`) are implemented with one generic `startResize(corner)` handler that fixes whichever edges the corner name doesn't mention and moves the other two, rather than four bespoke handlers or keeping only the original bottom-right handle.
**Rationale:** The task brief explicitly asks for "resize handles on the corners" (plural). A single parametrised handler generalises cleanly from the corner's two-character name (`"se"` → move the south and east edges) instead of duplicating the bottom-right-only math four times.

**Decision:** The "Page X of Y" indicator and the "Place on page" dropdown both track a `viewPage` state derived from the scroll container's `scrollTop` (via a `scroll` listener comparing it against each page's cumulative offset), not from the signature box's own page. Selecting a page in the dropdown calls `scrollTo({ top, behavior: 'smooth' })`; it does not move the signature box.
**Rationale:** The task brief describes the selector as a navigation aid ("changing page scrolls the PDF to that page"), separate from where the box itself has been dragged — a user might want to look at page 3 to decide where to place the box without that glance relocating a box they'd already positioned on page 1. The box's own page is still whatever page it's currently been dragged onto (re-derived from its centre point on every drag/resize, unchanged from the pre-redesign logic), and that — not the viewed page — is what's sent as `page` to `POST /invoices/{id}/sign`.

**Decision:** The `.kt-sign-*` CSS (previously living inside `settings.css`, apparently left over from having been built in the same pass as other settings work) was moved into a new, dedicated `frontend/src/styles/signing.css`, imported from `main.jsx` alongside the other stylesheets.
**Rationale:** Signing has no relationship to Settings; keeping its ~250 lines of full-screen-overlay CSS in a same-named file made `settings.css` a confusing place to look for either feature. This redesign already required touching essentially every rule in that block, making it a natural point to relocate it.

**Decision:** `InvoiceSignRequest`'s existing field names (`x`, `y`, `width`, `height`, `page` — already percentages of the page, per their own `Field(..., description=...)` text) were kept as-is rather than renamed to this task's literal `x_percent`/`y_percent`/`width_percent`/`height_percent`/`page_number`.
**Rationale:** The values these fields carry already satisfy the requirement (0–100 percentages of page width/height, already validated by `ge=0, le=100` constraints) — the task's field names describe the semantics the endpoint needed to have, which it already did. Renaming would touch the schema, the router's keyword arguments, and the frontend's `invoicesApi.sign()` payload for no behavioural change, and risked being mistaken for a fix that had actually happened, when the real fix was entirely on the frontend rendering side (see above).

**Decision:** `RequireAuth`, `RequireGuest`, `RequireAdmin`, and `DefaultRedirect` (`frontend/src/components/RouteGuards.jsx`) no longer redirect to `/setup` when `setupRequired` is true — only `RequireSetupNeeded` (which gates the `/setup` route itself) still reads that flag. `LoginPage.jsx` now reads `setupRequired` from `useAuth()` and shows a banner ("Welcome to Keep Track. No accounts exist yet." + a **Set up Keep Track** button that navigates to `/setup`) when it's true.
**Rationale:** Directly implements this task's requirement that Login is always the default landing page and Setup is only reached by an explicit action. `SetupPage.jsx` already redirected to `/login` (not the Dashboard) on completion from an earlier build — re-checked directly and confirmed no change was needed there, only the guards that had been *automatically routing past* Login before a user got the chance to see it.

**Tested:** Ran `vite build` after each stage of edits — clean throughout. Verified against the running dev containers (Vite HMR picked up every change live; one transient Babel parse error appeared in the frontend container's logs mid-edit from an unbalanced JSX fragment left by an intermediate `InvoiceDetailPage.jsx` edit, resolved by the very next save in the same sequence — confirmed gone from the logs afterwards). All browser testing below used a real Chrome install driven headlessly via `playwright-core` (matching prior sessions' approach), logged in as Superadmin.

Confirmed the actual root-cause fix end-to-end, not just visually: generated a throwaway 2-page test PDF via `fitz`, drove the full upload → review → sign flow, dragged the placement box to a specific position and resized it via the new SE corner handle, then read back the box's and the target page canvas's real `getBoundingClientRect()`s to compute the *expected* placement percentage completely independently of React's own internal state. After placing the signature and downloading the signed PDF, re-opened it with `fitz` on the backend container and read back the actual inserted image's bounding box: the left edge and width matched the independently-computed expected percentage to within 0.01 percentage points (18.235% expected vs. 18.24% actual; 40.12% width both). The vertical offset was consistently a few percent lower than expected, traced to `page.insert_image(..., keep_proportion=True)` centering a signature stroke (whose drawn aspect ratio is much wider/thinner than its box) vertically within the box's top 72% signature strip rather than top-aligning it — a sensible rendering choice, not a coordinate bug, and unrelated to the box's own left/top/width tracking. Also confirmed the "Signed: 2026-08-05" date text landed on the correct page (page 2, matching where the box was dragged) via `page.get_text()`.

Also verified: the page selector dropdown scrolls to the chosen page and updates the "Page X of Y" indicator correctly for both pages of the test PDF; the mobile drawer (390×844 viewport, `hasTouch: true`) opens showing all toolbar fields, collapses to a small "Signature tools" tab leaving the full PDF (both pages) visible, and re-expands correctly; opening the signing panel from `InvoiceDetailPage` (an already-signed invoice, so the button read "Re-sign and export") opened the same full-screen overlay, and **Cancel** closed it back to the detail page with no console errors. The only browser console message observed across every run was the pre-existing, unrelated `favicon.ico` 404 already noted in earlier entries in this log.

For the login/setup flow: `/`, `/login`, `/register`, and `/setup` were all exercised against the real dev containers (setup already completed there, `setup_required: false`) — visiting `/` while logged out landed on `/login` (not `/setup`); no banner was shown (correct, since setup is done); `/register` loaded; visiting `/setup` directly redirected to `/login`. The banner-shown / fresh-install path (`setup_required: true`) could not be exercised against that same database without deleting the real Admin account (`danewir`) a previous session had created there, which this task had no reason to destroy — instead, a fully isolated, throwaway `docker compose` stack was spun up (`-p kt-test-setup`, its own Postgres/backend/frontend containers and volumes, host ports 3001/8001, backend/frontend source bind-mounted read-write from the same checkout so it ran this task's actual code, not a stale image), migrated from empty with `alembic upgrade head`, and torn down completely (`down -v`) afterwards. Against that fresh instance: `/` landed on `/login` showing the "No accounts exist yet" banner; clicking **Set up Keep Track** opened `/setup`; completing the wizard (account, QR step, "Skip for now" on the start-date step) landed back on `/login` with the banner now gone; logging in with the new Admin's credentials correctly routed to `/mfa` (Admin accounts still require TOTP, unaffected by this task); visiting `/setup` again afterwards redirected away since setup was now done. Confirmed via `docker ps` and a direct query against the real dev database afterwards that the isolated stack's teardown left the primary `keeptrack-*` containers, volumes, and the `danewir` Admin account completely untouched.

Four throwaway test invoices created against the shared dev database during the coordinate-mapping testing (ids 16–19, suppliers "Test Supplier Ltd" / "Mobile Test Supplier" / "Desktop Shot Supplier") were soft-deleted via the API afterwards, consistent with this log's established test-data-cleanup convention.

## 2026-08-05 — Fix PDF.js canvas conflict in the signing panel

**Decision:** Root-caused the "Cannot use the same canvas during multiple render() operations" error to `SigningPanel.jsx`'s page-rendering effect (`renderAll`, keyed on `[numPages, containerWidth]`), not to the upload preview. That preview (`ReviewCard.jsx`) hasn't used pdf.js at all since the 2026-08-04 rotation-bug fix — it's a server-rendered `<img>` — so the two are already fully isolated; the conflict was entirely internal to `SigningPanel`. pdf.js tracks "is this canvas mid-render" in a single static `WeakSet` keyed by the canvas element (`InternalRenderTask.#canvasInUse`, confirmed directly by reading `pdf.mjs`) and throws exactly this error if a second `page.render()` targets a canvas still in that set. `renderAll` renders every page sequentially in a `for` loop, but its cleanup only flipped a local `cancelled` flag checked *after* the loop finished — it never called pdf.js's own `RenderTask.cancel()`, and the loop itself never checked `cancelled` between iterations. Once the PDF's pages laid out and the scroll container's vertical scrollbar appeared, `ResizeObserver` (watching that same container) fired again, `containerWidth` changed by a few pixels, and the effect re-ran — starting a second `renderAll` pass over the *same* `<canvas>` elements while the first pass was potentially still mid-render on a later page. That's the exact scenario the task brief described ("entering the signing flow after the upload preview"): the second pass is what actually threw, and it was reliably reproducible on first load of any PDF tall enough to trigger a scrollbar, not something upload-preview interference caused.
**Rationale:** Confirming the mechanism first (rather than guessing) is what showed the fix belonged entirely in `SigningPanel`'s own render lifecycle — no upload-preview isolation work was needed since none existed to begin with.

**Decision:** `renderPdfPage` (`frontend/src/utils/pdf.js`) no longer awaits `page.render(...).promise` internally — it returns `{ width, height, renderTask }` as soon as the render has *started*, handing the `RenderTask` back to the caller. `SigningPanel` now keeps one `RenderTask | null` per page index in a `renderTasksRef`, and before rendering into a given canvas it cancels whatever task is already tracked for that index (`prevTask.cancel()`) — synchronously, before the new `page.render()` call ever reaches pdf.js's `initializeGraphics()` (where the `WeakSet` check lives). The `renderAll` loop also now checks `cancelled` on every iteration (not just after the loop), and a `RenderingCancelledException` from an awaited `renderTask.promise` is treated as an expected, silent supersession rather than a real error (previously it would have surfaced as "Could not preview this PDF").
**Rationale:** This is the only sequencing that's actually safe against pdf.js's real (static, cross-document) reentrancy guard — a boolean "don't setState" flag was never going to prevent two `RenderTask`s from targeting one canvas, only cancelling the earlier one before the later one starts does that.

**Decision:** Each page's `<canvas>` is now keyed by `` `${docId}-${i}` `` (`docId` is a ref-backed counter bumped every time the `file` prop changes) instead of just the page index `i`, and the PDF-loading effect now proactively cancels all render tasks and destroys the previous `pdfRef.current` document at the *start* of its run (in addition to its cleanup), before bumping `docId`.
**Rationale:** Directly implements the task brief's "unique canvas key per render context" requirement, so that if `SigningPanel` were ever reused across documents without a full unmount (it currently isn't — see below), React would still tear down and recreate the canvas DOM nodes rather than handing a new document's renders the previous document's canvases. Belt-and-braces alongside the `RenderTask` cancellation, not a fix for a bug this app can currently hit.

**Not changed:** The "switching pages via the page selector" part of the task brief doesn't apply literally — per the 2026-08-05 full-screen signing UI entry above, all pages are already rendered up front (so the signature box can be dragged to any of them across one continuous scroll), and the page selector only calls `scrollTo()`, it never triggers a new render. Re-architecting that into per-page lazy rendering would be a much larger change than this bug needs and would regress the drag-across-pages UX that redesign deliberately built. Similarly, no code changes were needed for "upload preview isolation" (see above) or "revoke object URLs on close" — `SigningPanel` never creates a long-lived object URL for the PDF itself (it reads `file` via `arrayBuffer()`); the one object URL it does create, for the downloaded signed PDF, was already synchronously revoked right after triggering the download, before and after this fix.

**Tested:** Ran `vite build` — clean. Verified against the running dev containers with a real Chromium install driven via `playwright-core` (`chromium.launch` against the cached `ms-playwright` binary), logged in as Superadmin. Generated two throwaway test PDFs with `fitz` (a 2-page and a 1-page invoice) and drove the full flow: batch-uploaded both, filled in the fields Claude couldn't extract (AI extraction fails and degrades to empty fields with the placeholder `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` currently in `.env` — expected, unrelated to this fix, confirmed via the backend's `401 invalid x-api-key` log lines), continued to sign invoice 1, and — specifically to stress the resize race this bug depended on — cycled the viewport size four times while the pages were still laying out before drawing a signature and clicking **Place signature**. Zero console errors at every checkpoint. The flow then automatically returned to invoice 2's review card; repeated fill → continue to sign → **Cancel** → re-enter signing → draw → place for invoice 2, again with zero console errors throughout. The only console message observed across the whole run was the pre-existing, unrelated `favicon.ico` 404 already noted in the 2026-08-05 full-screen signing UI entry. Confirmed end-to-end (not just UI) by querying the dev database directly afterwards: both invoices had `reviewed = true`, `signed = true`, and a non-null `signed_pdf_path`. All four resulting test invoices (two per run, across two script iterations) were soft-deleted via the API afterwards, consistent with this log's test-data-cleanup convention.

## 2026-08-05 — Expected month picker fix on Projects page

**Decision:** `ProjectsPage.jsx`'s add/edit form no longer uses `<input type="month">` for a planned project's expected month. It's replaced with two plain `<select>` dropdowns (full month name; year = current year plus the next 5) that combine into a `YYYY-MM-01` string on submit, with a plain-text preview (e.g. "June 2026") shown beneath. Defaults to the current month/year when the add form opens; the edit form pre-fills both selects by splitting the project's existing `expected_month`.
**Rationale:** The reported bug ("date picker cannot be selected, typing a date manually gives a validation error") is `<input type="month">`'s well-known poor cross-browser support — pre-16.4 Safari (and other WebKit-based browsers) renders it as a plain text field with no calendar affordance and its own strict native format enforcement, which is exactly what the two symptoms describe. Two independent `<select>` elements have no such native-parsing pitfalls in any browser, which is also literally what the task brief asked for.

**Decision:** `expected_month_label` (used by the dashboard's planned-projects panel, `ForecastBreakdownPage`, the PDF report's planned-projects row, and the AI report narrative) now renders as `"June 2026"` (full month name) instead of `"Jun 2026"`. A new `MONTH_LABELS_FULL` list was added alongside the existing `MONTH_LABELS` in `financial_year_service.py` — `MONTH_LABELS` itself was left untouched since it's also used for abbreviated FY range labels (e.g. "FY2025/26 (Sep–Aug)") and chart month labels, which are intentionally short and out of scope for this fix.
**Rationale:** Found while testing the picker fix end-to-end: the Projects page list already showed the full month name via the frontend's own `formatMonthYear()`, but the dashboard panel (which uses the backend-supplied `expected_month_label` rather than formatting the raw date itself) showed the abbreviated form — an inconsistency the task explicitly asked to be fixed ("show the same format" in both places).

**Not changed (backend create/update validation):** `POST /projects` and `PUT /projects/{id}` already accepted `expected_month` as an ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` string (a Pydantic `date` field) and already returned a clear `422` with a field-level message on a malformed value, and already normalised any submitted date to the 1st of its month server-side (`docs/decisions-log.md`, 2026-08-04 planned projects build entry). Verified directly against the running backend rather than assuming — no backend validation changes were needed.

**Tested:** Built the frontend (`vite build`, clean) and restarted the backend container to pick up the Python changes. Drove the real app with a headless Chromium (`playwright-core`, same approach as prior sessions in this log) against the running dev containers, logged in as Superadmin: opened the add-project form and confirmed it defaulted to the current month/year (August 2026) with a live "August 2026" preview; added a project for June 2026 and confirmed it saved and displayed as "Expected June 2026" on the Projects list with no validation error; edited that project, confirmed the month/year selects were correctly pre-filled (June/2026), changed it to December 2026, and confirmed the list updated to "Expected December 2026"; confirmed the dashboard's Planned projects panel showed the same project as "Expected December 2026" (previously would have shown "Dec 2026") after the backend restart. Also verified directly via `curl`: `POST /projects` with `expected_month: "not-a-date"` returns `422` with a clear per-field message, and a mid-month date (`"2026-06-15"`) is correctly normalised to `"2026-06-01"` on save. No console errors during the browser session other than the pre-existing, unrelated `favicon.ico` 404 already noted elsewhere in this log. Both projects created during testing were deactivated via the API afterwards, consistent with this log's test-data-cleanup convention.

## 2026-08-05 — Branding refinement and dashboard graph size reduction

**Decision:** `Header.jsx`'s brand unit was restructured into two visually distinct groups inside the same `<Link>`: a `.kt-brand-logo-group` (logo icon + "Keep Track" wordmark, tightly spaced at `gap: 8px`) and, only when an instance name is set, a `.kt-brand-instance` group (a 1px `.kt-brand-divider` line in `var(--kt-border)`, then the instance name in `#5F5E5A`, 14px, medium weight). The wordmark itself now uses `.kt-wordmark-keep`/`.kt-wordmark-track` classes instead of inline `style` colour props. The previous version ran the instance name inline inside the wordmark span as `" — {siteName}"` text, which read as part of the wordmark rather than a distinct, secondary element.
**Rationale:** Directly implements this task's brief — logo+wordmark as one cohesive unit, instance name visually separated by a divider rather than punctuation, and coloured as a clearly secondary element. The `showSiteName` logic (hide when unset or still the literal default `"Keep Track"`) and the mobile hide-behaviour were both kept exactly as they were — only the presentation changed, not the condition.

**Decision:** `FinancialChart.jsx`'s SVG `HEIGHT` constant went from `320` to `196` (a 38.75% reduction, inside the brief's requested 30-40% range), with `MARGIN` tightened from `{top: 16, bottom: 32}` to `{top: 10, bottom: 24}`. `left`/`right` margins were left untouched since they're sized for the currency-label text, not the chart's height.
**Rationale:** The chart is a hand-built inline SVG scaled by `viewBox` (see the 2026-08-04 main dashboard build entry) — its rendered height is purely a function of these two constants, so this was the entire fix for "the chart is too tall."

**Decision:** Beyond the chart itself, several dashboard/layout spacing values were tightened to get the panels row's top edge to actually clear a 1080px viewport: `.kt-main` top padding (32→24px), `.kt-page-title`/`.kt-page-subtitle` bottom margins, `.kt-quick-actions` bottom margin, `.kt-metric-row` bottom margin, `.kt-dashboard-panel` padding (24px all sides → 16px 24px) and bottom margin (24→14px), `.kt-panel-subtitle` bottom margin, and the chart's own legend margin. `.kt-metric-card`'s own padding was deliberately left untouched.
**Rationale:** The task brief explicitly said "Metric cards row at the top — keep as is," so that component's internal sizing was off-limits even though trimming it would have helped the fit — only the spacing *around* it (the row's own margin) was adjusted. Measured directly (not eyeballed): before this pass, the panels row's top edge sat at document y≈1181px against a 1080px-tall viewport (playwright, logged in as Superadmin against the real dev containers) — 101px past the fold even before accounting for any real browser chrome. After tightening, it sits at y≈1051px, clearing a full 1080px viewport with the entire target-reserve gauge also fully visible above it, not just its top edge.

**Not addressed:** A "typical 1080p laptop screen" in practice has less usable height than a bare 1080px viewport once browser chrome (tab/address bar, bookmarks bar) and OS chrome (menu bar, dock/taskbar) are accounted for — no CSS change here can guarantee the fit holds in every real browser window at every zoom/scaling level, only that it now clears a full, chrome-free 1080px viewport with room to spare. A more structural change (e.g. a two-column chart/gauge layout) would close that gap further but was judged out of scope for a spacing-tightening pass.

**Tested:** Ran `vite build` after each stage of edits — clean throughout. Verified against the running dev containers with a real headless Chromium (`playwright`, downloaded fresh into a scratch directory for this session), logged in as Superadmin (no MFA, per the existing Superadmin bootstrap). Confirmed via direct DOM measurement (not just a screenshot) that the panels row's top edge moved from y≈1181px to y≈1051px against a 1920×1080 viewport. Screenshotted the dashboard at 1920×1080 (chart, all four metric cards, the full target-reserve gauge, and the top of all three panels — Upcoming, Planned projects, Recent activity — all visible with no scrolling) and the header at a 375px mobile width (instance name correctly hidden, logo+wordmark unchanged). Also screenshotted the header at 1440px wide with an instance name set ("Stayton Road KHOC") to confirm the divider and instance-name styling render as intended.

## 2026-08-05 — Instance name resize and move to General settings

**Decision:** `.kt-instance-name` went from 14px/500 weight to 20px — the same font-size as `.kt-wordmark` — at 400 weight, keeping the existing `#5F5E5A` mid-grey. `.kt-brand-divider`'s height went from 20px to 28px to stay proportionate to the larger text.
**Rationale:** The previous 14px size (set in the 2026-08-05 branding refinement entry above) read as too subordinate next to the wordmark once actually viewed side-by-side. Matching the wordmark's font-size while keeping the weight light and the colour muted keeps the instance name legible without competing with "Keep Track" for visual weight — same intent as before, recalibrated.

**Decision:** The site/instance name field moved from Settings → Terminology to Settings → General (new "Instance name" row, above "App start date"), and `GET /settings/terminology` / `TerminologyOut` / `TerminologyUpdate` no longer carry `site_name` at all — it's returned only by the existing general `GET /settings` endpoint, like every other non-terminology setting. A new `SiteNameContext`/`useSiteName()` (`frontend/src/context/SiteNameContext.jsx`), fetching `GET /settings` and reading the `site_name` row, replaces `TerminologyContext`'s former `site_name` field as `Header.jsx`'s source for the instance name.
**Rationale:** `site_name` was never a `term_*` label — it's a display setting, not app vocabulary — and grouping it with Terminology was a mismatch this task corrects. Splitting it into its own context (rather than folding it into `TerminologyContext`) keeps `GET /settings/terminology` scoped to only the five `term_*` labels it now returns, and keeps the two settings' independent save/refresh cycles (Terminology's "Save all"/"Reset to defaults" vs. General's per-field "Save") from being coupled through one shared piece of state.

**Decision:** The General settings "Instance name" field is optional and, when cleared to blank, saves the literal string `"Keep Track"` via `PUT /settings/site_name` rather than clearing the row to `NULL` via `DELETE /settings/site_name`.
**Rationale:** `services/settings_service.get_site_name()` returns `None` (not the `"Keep Track"` fallback) when the row exists with a `NULL` value — that fallback only ever triggered for a genuinely *missing* row. `NULL` would reach `pdf_report_service`'s cover-page `Paragraph(site_name, ...)` and likely error. `"Keep Track"` is already the header's own "nothing custom set" sentinel (`Header.jsx`'s `showSiteName` check), so writing it explicitly avoids the `NULL` path entirely without needing to fix `get_site_name()` itself, which is out of scope for a UI-relocation task.

**Tested:** Ran `vite build` — clean. Verified against the running dev containers with headless Chromium (Playwright), logged in as Superadmin: set an instance name via Settings → General, confirmed the header updated immediately (no reload) at the new 20px/400-weight/`#5F5E5A` styling alongside the 20px/600-weight wordmark (computed styles read directly from the DOM), confirmed Settings → Terminology no longer shows any site/instance name field, and reset `site_name` back to `"Keep Track"` via the API afterwards to leave the instance's actual settings unchanged.
